# Paper and Slate skin contrast

Generated by `scripts/check-current-contrast.ts` from an isolated fictional PostgreSQL fixture and Chromium rendering.

- Stylesheet: `public/styles.css`

- Browser scanner SHA-256: `2272f5c7978ccb44b69d37e0b804cb940aef78195cd98878abfc5c68ad205a87`
- Browser: Chromium 156.0.8078.4
- Unique semantic/color pairs: 604; failing: 0; uncovered: 0.
- Comparison uses the unrounded ratio. Displayed ratios are rounded to six decimal places.
- Normal text: 4.5:1; large text and essential icons, identifying control borders, focus indicators: 3:1.
- Alpha colors are composited in sRGB over the actual painted background before relative luminance. Disabled controls and decorative separators are reported as exemptions, never passes.
- Explicit light/dark modes are each checked against both OS schemes. Native browser controls retain platform rendering; their authored text, fill, border and accent are included, with internal glyph coverage documented below.

## Scope

The audit covers actual DOM text and placeholders, selected text, authored control boundaries, D-pad icons, disclosure markers, keyboard focus, selected tabs, progress fills, hover and pressed button combinations. Error, invalid and toast cases use clearly marked test-only DOM fixtures. Feedback uses the real local UI with intercepted fictional error and delayed-success responses, including empty, invalid, error, sending and sent states. Open modal scans exclude inert background controls; neutral native checkbox references are inserted inside the modal top layer. Empty and populated views use separate fictional players. Policy redirects are checked without fetching third-party destinations. No production API credentials, data or financial/game/auth calculations are changed.

Dormant legacy `.goal`, `.coin`, `.ladder`, `.quest` and `.money` selectors are not represented as rendered UI. Decorative container/table separators and aria-hidden goal/controller ornaments are measured and listed as exemptions. Native calendar/month/select/checkbox glyph colors are sampled from actual Chromium element screenshots. Chromium's visited-color privacy protection is handled by requiring unconditional authored link colors and rejecting visited-specific overrides; otherwise coverage is explicitly uncovered. No unsupported authored background is silently accepted.
